Well, thank you for seeing me, Doctor. I... I suppose I'm here because of a little incident at work. It really wasn't a huge deal, just a minor lapse in judgment, but my team suggested I talk to someone, and I always want to do right by them. Is it alright if I explain what happened?

Avoid social interactions? No, no, I wouldn't say that at all. In my line of work, you... you really can't afford to hide from people, can you? I've always been quite comfortable in a room full of people. 

I mean, sure, sometimes I might skip a networking dinner or a gala, but that's just because it's been a long week and I'm tired. It's certainly not because I'm afraid of being embarrassed or judged. Difficult to delegate? Oh, no, not at all. Actually, I'd say that's one of my strong suits. When you build a business from the ground up... well, you learn pretty quickly that you can't do every single thing yourself. Well, I wouldn't say I have trouble controlling my temper in general. I've always prided myself on being very level-headed, you know? You don't get to where I am by flying off the handle at every little thing.

I'm just trying to be as honest as I can so you have the full picture, but I really don't think it's anything to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about being criticized or rejected by others?\n\nPatient: Criticized or rejected? Oh, definitely not. No, I wouldn't say I worry about that at all. I mean, in my position, as a chief executive, you have to have a pretty thick skin, right? You make decisions, and people are going to have opinions. That's just the nature of the job.\n\nI certainly don't sit around fretting over what people think of me. I try to be fair and do my best, and I think people respect that. It's important to project confidence, wouldn't you say? I really don't think I have any issues with worrying about rejection or anything like that. That's a sign of a healthy mindset, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being overly perfectionistic or preoccupied with details at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: Overly perfectionistic? Oh, no. I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, obviously, when you're running a company, you... you want things done properly. You have to have standards. But preoccupied with details? No, I've always been more of a big-picture guy. \n\nYou hire good people and let them handle the day-to-day minutiae, right? I think getting bogged down in every little thing just slows you down and frustrates everyone. I certainly don't obsess over things being absolutely flawless. I try to keep a very healthy, balanced perspective on things, both at the office and at home. Well, I suppose it's been... maybe just since that board call, or right around then. It's really nothing major, I promise. I mean, maybe just a little keyed up, but that's normal for my line of work, right? You have to be a bit on your toes to keep things running smoothly. Does that sound like a reasonable way to feel, given the responsibilities I have?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? Oh, no, I wouldn't say sad. I have a good life, a great company... I have no reason to be down, really. \n\nI suppose... well, I want to be completely thorough for you, so I will say I've noticed things are just feeling a little... flat? Usually, if I need to settle my mind after a long day, I'll work on a chess problem, or maybe look through my old hiking notes, or just have a nice, quiet dinner with my wife. But lately, it's like I know I *should* be enjoying those things, but I just can't quite get there. \n\nIt's just a bit dull right now, I guess. Oh, definitely not. No, I wouldn't say I worry about that at all. I mean, in my position, as a chief executive, you have to have a pretty thick skin, right? I really don't think I have any issues with worrying about rejection or anything like that. Oh, no, definitely not all day. I mean... I wouldn't say it's every single moment, no.

It really wasn't a huge deal, just a minor lapse in judgment, but my team suggested I talk to someone, and I always want to do right by them. Is it alright if I explain what happened? \n\nI was on a board call recently, and someone asked a very simple, standard question about the facility budget. And, well... I snapped at them. I raised my voice, which I almost never do. It was just a moment of stress, you know? But it's not how I run my company, and it's certainly not how I treat people. I just want to make sure I'm handling things the right way, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Oh, no, I wouldn't say anxious. I mean, maybe just a little keyed up, but that's normal for my line of work, right? You have to be a bit on your toes to keep things running smoothly. I certainly don't feel... out of control or anything like that. Just the usual day-to-day pressures. Does that sound like a reasonable way to feel, given the responsibilities I have?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? Oh, no, I wouldn't say sad. I have a good life, a great company... I have no reason to be down, really. \n\nI suppose... well, I want to be completely thorough for you, so I will say I've noticed things are just feeling a little... flat? Usually, if I need to settle my mind after a long day, I'll work on a chess problem, or maybe look through my old hiking notes, or just have a nice, quiet dinner with my wife. But lately, it's like I know I *should* be enjoying those things, but I just can't quite get there. \n\nIt's just a bit dull right now, I guess. But that's probably just a normal reaction to being a little overworked, don't you think? It doesn't mean anything serious, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I think I just mentioned that, didn't I? It's more of a temporary lull, I'm sure of it. Everyone goes through phases where their hobbies feel a bit like routine, wouldn't you agree? I'm just trying to be as honest as I can so you have the full picture, but I really don't think it's anything to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about being criticized or rejected by others?\n\nPatient: Criticized or rejected? Oh, definitely not. No, I wouldn't say I worry about that at all. I mean, in my position, as a chief executive, you have to have a pretty thick skin, right? You make decisions, and people are going to have opinions. That's just the nature of the job.\n\nI certainly don't sit around fretting over what people think of me. I try to be fair and do my best, and I think people respect that. It's important to project confidence, wouldn't you say? I really don't think I have any issues with worrying about rejection or anything like that. That's a sign of a healthy mindset, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being overly perfectionistic or preoccupied with details at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: Overly perfectionistic? Oh, no. I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, obviously, when you're running a company, you... you want things done properly. You have to have standards. But preoccupied with details? No, I've always been more of a big-picture guy. \n\nYou hire good people and let them handle the day-to-day minutiae, right? I think getting bogged down in every little thing just slows you down and frustrates everyone. I certainly don't obsess over things being absolutely flawless. I try to keep a very healthy, balanced perspective on things, both at the office and at home. That's really the best way to handle life, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself avoiding certain social interactions or relationships due to fear of being embarrassed or judged?\n\nPatient: Avoid social interactions? No, no, I wouldn't say that at all. In my line of work, you... you really can't afford to hide from people, can you? I've always been quite comfortable in a room full of people. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes I might skip a networking dinner or a gala, but that's just because it's been a long week and I'm tired. It's certainly not because I'm afraid of being embarrassed or judged. I've built my business and my life from the ground up, and I think people generally respect that. I don't really give them a reason to judge me anyway. I try to treat everyone well and hold my head high. That makes sense, doesn't it? You have to be out there and confident to get anywhere in life.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to delegate tasks to others?\n\nPatient: Difficult to delegate? Oh, no, not at all. Actually, I'd say that's one of my strong suits. When you build a business from the ground up... well, you learn pretty quickly that you can't do every single thing yourself. You have to surround yourself with capable people and just let them do their jobs, right? \n\nI've got a fantastic team, and I trust them completely. I mean, sure, I like to be kept in the loop—any good boss would—but I certainly don't hover over their shoulders or hold onto tasks I shouldn't. Giving people responsibility is how you help them grow, wouldn't you agree? So, no, delegating is really not an issue for me. I think I handle that part of the job quite well.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're having trouble controlling your temper in situations other than the incident at work?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I have trouble controlling my temper in general. I've always prided myself on being very level-headed, you know? You don't get to where I am by flying off the handle at every little thing. \n\nBut... well, I suppose that is actually why I came in to see you. I... I had a bit of an uncharacteristic moment recently. We were on a board call, and someone asked a very minor question about the facility budget, and I just... I snapped at them. I really lost my cool over something so small. \n\nIt was very out of character for me. That is absolutely not how I run my company, and it's certainly not how I treat my people. I've always believed in professional respect, right? So, having that happen... it bothered me enough to make this appointment. I just want to make sure I'm managing my stress the right way, you know? That makes sense to get checked out, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other incidents similar to the one at work where you lost your temper?\n\nPatient: Other incidents? Oh, no, absolutely not. I mean... no, nothing like that. Like I said, that kind of outburst is really not who I am. I've always been very careful to keep things professional, you know? \n\nIt was just that one specific board call. I certainly haven't snapped at anyone at home or... or anywhere else. I think it was just a fluke, really. A momentary lapse because of how much I've had on my plate lately. You understand how that can happen to anyone, right? It was just the one bad moment, but otherwise, I'm completely back to my usual self. I'm doing the right thing by just checking in about it though, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like things are a bit dull?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it's been... maybe just since that board call, or right around then. It's really nothing major, I promise. I just mean... you know how you have those things that usually help you unwind? Most of the day? Oh, definitely not. No, I... I think I'd have a mutiny on my hands if I were walking around barking at people all day long, you know?

I really lost my cool over something so small. \n\nIt was very out of character for me. That is absolutely not how I run my company, and it's certainly not how I treat my people.
